# Older-coin paper project — frozen specification `older-8`
_20 Nov 2026 (UK). Paper only. Every number below is a constant in `src/spec.mjs`; changing one is a new spec version._
_Agreed between the owner, ChatGPT (reviewer) and Claude (implementer). older-3 (20 Nov, afternoon) widens the scope to Solana-wide and records three rule amendments; the strategy definitions are otherwise those of older-2._

## Shared rules
- **Population: Solana-wide, launchpad- and exchange-neutral** — any token of eligible age and valuation that our providers can observe and quote. Two observation methods, always labelled and never merged silently:
  - **PumpSwap · live:** pump.fun coins on their canonical SOL-quoted PumpSwap pool, observed from every decoded pool event; fills from the reconstructed pool state.
  - **Solana-wide · sampled:** every other token (Raydium, Meteora, Orca, other launchpads, non-SOL pools reachable by a route from SOL), observed by **sampled** prices at a recorded cadence; fills from **routed quotes** for the exact size. A mint is observed by one method only (live wins).
  - "Solana-wide" is broad coverage, not every mint. Unsupported, unpriced and unroutable tokens are counted.
- **Age at entry:** 8 hours to 90 days since token creation. Sources are labelled: on-chain/back-filled creation for the live method; for the sampled method the provider's **first-pool creation time, a lower bound on token age** (it can understate age, never overstate it — the 8-hour minimum cannot be broken by it; the age group may be understated). Crossing 90 days never closes an open position.
- **Age groups:** A1 8–16 h · A2 16–24 h · A3 24–36 h · A4 36 h–90 d.
- **Entry valuation:** $40,000–$1,000,000 at the signal. Definition is recorded on every entry: live = pool price × on-chain base supply; sampled = provider market cap (circulating supply × price). Fully diluted value is never mixed in.
- **Stake:** 0.2 SOL. Cost of an entry = 0.2 SOL + 0.0005 SOL (declared transaction-cost assumption, charged on every execution leg of every simulated book).
- **One entry per mint, family and entry-age group.** Families whose signals fire on the **same update** share one simulated entry.
- **Price (live method):** pool spot price from **effective reserves** (quote vault + signed `virtual_quote_reserves`) after each reserve-changing event (buy, sell, deposit, withdraw, boost).
- **USD:** every observation is converted at the SOL/USD of its own moment (never re-priced later). Costs, exits, trails, stall thresholds and P&L are in SOL. A stale USD reference suspends new entries; positions are still managed.
- **Clock:** our receive time (for a quote: when the response became available). Block time is stored alongside, never used to back-date. Held/replayed events never create signals.
- **Observations:** a transaction is atomic, so all of its events on a pool yield ONE observation — the committed state after the last of them. Intermediate states are never prices, peaks or triggers.
- **Coverage:** a rule is evaluated only when its whole window is known. Coverage follows evidence: an empty back-fill proves nothing; an outage repaired from minute bars counts for hour-scale windows only, never for a fresh crossing, FAST or SECOND-WAVE; a pool-reserve discontinuity restarts the coin's history. Feed outages, restarts, a stale USD reference, a hole in a token's samples (more than 3× its cadence) or a token omitted by the price provider all break coverage.
- **Risk evidence** (mint/freeze authority, Token-2022 extensions, provider markers) is recorded on every token. Only a known transfer restriction (transfer hook, non-transferable, permanent delegate, default-frozen accounts, a non-zero transfer fee, pausable/confidential) stops entries, because the paper book's plain buy-and-sell assumption would not hold. Missing risk data is recorded as missing, not as clean. No other filter is added.

## Execution model (declared assumptions, identical for every family)
- **Buy:** scheduled at signal + 1 s. Priced from the pool state **in force at that moment** (the latest reconstructed state — no other trader has to trade). If that state is not trustworthy, the first trustworthy state within 5 s is used and the delay recorded; otherwise the entry is cancelled as a **pricing/data timeout** (not "untradeable").
- **Sell:** scheduled at trigger + 0.7 s, priced the same way. A triggered sell is never dropped: it waits for a trustworthy state and records the real delay. After 24 h it is shown as unresolved exposure but stays pending and still closes when a price returns. A state can only price a moment at or after the time it was observed.
- **Fill maths:** constant product on effective reserves. Pool fees per side are measured from the pool's own latest buy/sell as the user's net debit/credit against the amount that moved on the curve (overlapping fee fields are never added together).
- **Entry check (diagnostic, live method):** one batched account read (pool + two vaults) per unique entry; recorded with slots and times; never replaces the simulated fill.
- **Routed-quote adapter (sampled method):** the quote is **requested at or after the scheduled time**; the fill time is when the response became available (never back-dated); route, router, fee fields and both timestamps are stored. Fees inside a quote are not charged again; only the declared transaction cost is added. One quote per distinct size (the 0.2 SOL buy; the position's full and remaining inventory) — never one per result row. No route: the buy is cancelled as a pricing/data timeout; an exit stays triggered and visible as unresolved exposure. A quote is an expected output, not a confirmed on-chain fill.

## Exit policies (every entry carries both)
- **trail:** trailing stop of 20 / 25 / 30 / 40 % on the after-cost liquidation value of the position; 24-hour maximum hold from the fill.
- **scale:** the same trail, plus: when the full position's after-cost value reaches 2× cost (and no full exit has triggered), sell **half the original tokens** 0.7 s later; trail the rest with the same percentage. Same 24-hour deadline. A trail trigger during the pending half-sale becomes one full sale (the same tokens are never sold twice).
  - **Formula.** `part` = ½ × original tokens. Half-sale proceeds = `sell(part) − 0.0005` (one leg, one cost). High-water mark: `peak_new = peak_old × value(remaining) ÷ value(held before)`, both valued on the state that prices the sale. So the position's drawdown from its high is **identical before and after** the half-sale: selling half neither looks like a 50% fall nor resets the trail. Final return = (half-sale proceeds + final-sale proceeds) ÷ cost − 1; the final sale pays its own 0.0005. A reset-the-trail variant would be a separate future experiment.

## Entry families (14)
| # | Family | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | AGE-DOUBLE | First fresh crossing of 2× the market cap at the start of the current age group (8 / 16 / 24 / 36 h). Reference must be known history; otherwise `reference_status=MISSING`, no entry. |
| 2 | LOW4H | Fresh crossing of 2× the lowest price of the preceding 4 hours. No pullback needed. |
| 3 | CONT | First eligible observation with price ≥ 1.30× the price 15 minutes earlier **and** ≥ 0.95× the preceding 1-hour high. Not a crossing. |
| 4 | SLOW-BUILD | LOW4H's first eligible candidate, accepted only if ≥ 30 min passed since the low and no completed 1-minute candle from the low's minute up to the trigger's minute gained more than 20% (exactly 20% allowed). Rejections recorded; no later attempt in that age group. Shares LOW4H's entry. |
| 5 | HH-BREAK | Price strictly above the preceding 60-minute high, and volume over **[B − 15 min, B)** ≥ 2× the median of sixteen adjacent 15-minute buckets covering **[B − 255 min, B − 15 min)**, where B is the last completed minute boundary (**amended in older-3**: half-open intervals, no overlap, no missing minute; the unfinished minute counts on neither side). Zero baseline: recorded, not entered. Needs a volume source: **not evaluated in the sampled method**. |
| 6 | CONT-STALL | CONT's exact entries; at fill + 60 min a surviving position is closed if its after-cost value never reached 1.15× cost. |
| 7 | LOW4H-LIQ25K | LOW4H's first eligible candidate, accepted only if pool liquidity ≥ $25,000 and the 0.2 SOL static price-impact diagnostic ≤ 5% (each test recorded separately). Shares LOW4H's entry. |
| 8–10 | FAST-1M / 3M / 5M | Fresh crossing of 2× the lowest pool spot price of the preceding 60 / 180 / 300 seconds (exact observations; the price standing at the window's start counts). |
| 11–13 | FAST150-1M / 3M / 5M | The same at 1.5×. |
| 15–17 | FAST-10M / FAST-30M / FAST150-10M | **Added in older-4 (21 Nov 2026, owner's decision):** the same rule over 10 and 30 minutes at 2×, and over 10 minutes at 1.5× — to fill the gap between the 5-minute and the 4-hour books. Same exact-observation coverage over the whole window. They do not seed SECOND-WAVE. Every FAST entry also records `move_s`, the seconds from the window's low to the trigger, so results can be split by speed afterwards. |
| 14 | SECOND-WAVE | Seeded by the first 2× FAST trigger of the 1-, 3- or 5-minute books. Track the running high; the first fall to ≤ 0.75× it freezes that high; buy on the first observation strictly above the frozen high. Expires 60 minutes after the seed. **Amended in older-3:** one active setup per mint, kept alive to its own expiry even across an age boundary; the seed uses the seed age group's attempt; the trade is classified by the age group **at entry**, whose slot must be free and is then used; seed age is recorded. Eligibility re-checked at the buy. |

17 families × 4 ages × 5 stops (20 / 25 / 30 / 40 / **60%**, the last added in older-5 on 21 Nov 2026 by the owner's decision) = **340 rows**, × 2 exit policies = **680 comparisons** (544 in older-4; 14 families and 448 comparisons up to older-3), viewable per population/method. They share coins, entries and stops: correlated comparisons, not independent results, and their sum is not a funded portfolio's return.

**What the sampled method can miss** (stated on the dashboard): FAST triggers, fresh crossings, trailing highs, stop crossings and SECOND-WAVE seeds/pullbacks/reclaims that happen between samples. FAST-1M on sampled tokens is evaluated on the samples that exist and is labelled sampled; it is not equivalent to the live books.

## Recorded with each entry (never used as filters)
Exact age, market-cap band, 15-minute rise, largest 1-minute candle of the last hour, 15-minute volume, buys/sells/unique and repeat buyers (15 min), liquidity, static impact, distance from the 1-hour high, 4-hour drawdown and recovery; the previous observation and threshold that made the crossing; for FAST/SECOND-WAVE every observation of the window; the trigger's signature, slot, block time, receive and evaluation times, reserves, fees, supply and SOL/USD; fill and exit delays; the entry check.

## Reporting
Per row: coins, filled/open/closed/cancelled/unresolved, net SOL, average and quantiles of after-cost return, win rate, losses of ≥ 40% (the project's severe-loss measure), ≥ 50% and ≥ 60%, result without the top three coins, first/second half, holding time, stake committed, runner outcomes (after-cost value reaching 2×/3×/5×/10× of cost within 24 h of the fill, including after an exit), startup entries (first 24 h after the first start; kept in the totals). Plus overlap between families, rejections and missing data, watched coins, feed freshness, disk, and running costs kept separate from paper returns. First review after seven days of entries.

## Recorded measurements at each entry (added 20 Nov 2026, 17:50 UK — background data only, NOT entry rules)
Owner's decision: collect more information without adding books. Nothing below is used to accept or reject an entry; the 448 books are unchanged.
- **From decoded trades (live method only)**, for the last 1, 3 and 5 minutes before the decision (`features.w1 / w3 / w5`): USD volume, number of buy and sell transactions, number of different buying wallets; for 5 minutes also the largest single buyer's share of the buy volume (`top_buyer_share`: near 1 = one wallet did the buying, low = many wallets).
- `vol60_usd` and `vol_surge5` = last 5 minutes ÷ the average 5 minutes of the 55 minutes before (only when a full hour of history exists).
- Already recorded before: 15-minute volume, buys, sells, different buyers, repeat buyers; liquidity; round-trip impact; rise over 15 minutes.
- **From Jupiter's token data, for every entry of either method** (`entry.snap`): holder count, top-holder percentage, and Jupiter's own 5-minute and 1-hour statistics exactly as returned (numeric fields), with request and reply times and the delay after the decision. Jupiter's field names are not verified from this workspace; they are stored as received. A token Jupiter does not return is recorded as missing.
- Sampled coins have no decoded trades, so their `w1 / w3 / w5` are empty; their 5-minute figures are Jupiter's.
- Use after the run: compare winners and losers on these numbers (for example holder rise of 10–15% in 5 minutes, or volume surge), and only then decide whether any becomes a rule.

## Data-trust rules added after the second independent review (20 Nov 2026, evening)
- **Pool fee:** measured only from validated instructions and only inside 0–10%; a failed measurement is counted and never used.
- **Reserve chain:** base side exact; quote side within 0.1% of effective reserves. A break restarts the coin's history and trust.
- **Unknown virtual reserves are not zero:** no state until an event carries them.
- **Boost events:** the rebuilt state is quarantined until the next swap.
- **Back-filled bars:** kept only when accepted, apart from live data; continuous only where consecutive bars are ≤ 5 minutes apart (a declared approximation for hour-scale windows). They are never read by a fresh crossing, FAST or SECOND-WAVE: those use exact observations only, continuous over the whole window. For hour-scale reads a newer back-filled close beats an older exact observation.
- **Boost quarantine** lasts through deposits and withdrawals until the next swap.
- **Sampled tokens:** no valuation without the provider's market cap or circulating supply; valid decimals required.
- **Routed half-sale:** its quotes must be requested within 5 s of each other.
- **Trade-window measurements:** only from exact observations covering the whole window.
- **Saving:** open positions at most 1 s behind, and all saved on a planned stop.
- **Carried prices:** a price is used at a later moment only if the whole stretch from its own time to that moment is known (exact use: unbroken exact observation; hour-scale use: covered history). A feed outage, a loss of the coin's data, a boost quarantine or a re-admission to the sampled list breaks the carry. Verified silence of any length does not.

## older-5 (21 Nov 2026, 13:30 UK — owner's decisions)

**1. A 60% trailing stop.** A fifth stop value beside 20 / 25 / 30 / 40%, under both exit policies, with exactly the same trigger and fill rules. 680 comparison rows. It exists only for entries made after the change (an earlier entry's price path after its 40% exit was not kept, so nothing is back-calculated).

**2. Stake views — display only.** The paper trade is and stays 0.2 SOL; no decision, trigger or stored result reads a stake view. The books page can re-price each trade at 0.5 / 1 / 2 / 5 SOL (`book.mjs atStake`):
- the pool reserves and sell fee are now saved with every pool-priced half-sale and exit (`half.pool`, `exit.pool`), as they already were with the buy; the latest pool an open position was valued on is kept as `mark`;
- the buy, the half-sale and the exit are re-priced with the same constant-product formulas on those saved reserves, one transaction cost per leg; at 0.2 SOL the result equals the recorded trade exactly (tested);
- declared limits: the moments are those of the 0.2 SOL trade (a larger position's stop would trigger slightly earlier); each leg is priced on the pool as observed, without the larger buy's own effect on it (the cautious side: on a flat price it understates the round trip by 2n/(Q+2n) for a buy of n SOL into effective reserves Q); other traders' reaction to a larger buy is unknowable. It is an estimate of size sensitivity, not a second simulation;
- a sale recorded before reserves were saved (the owner chose to keep the existing statistics rather than start a new database, and asked for an estimate): its pool is rebuilt from the sale itself — the constant-product pool with the buy pool's k on which the recorded tokens fetch the recorded SOL. Exact at 0.2 SOL; at other stakes off by however much k changed between buy and sale. Marked `est` and counted on the page;
- a routed-quote (sampled) trade, whose quotes were for the 0.2 SOL size, cannot be re-priced. It is not left out (that changed which trades a book held from one stake to the next): it keeps its 0.2 SOL percentage result on the larger stake, with no extra impact taken off, flagged `flat` and counted on the page. Every stake shows the same set of trades.

## Data-trust rules added after the sixth independent review (21 Nov 2026, 14:45 UK)

- **Loss and recovery are different moments.** When a coin's data is lost, its history restarts at the first valid observation that arrives afterwards, not at the moment of the loss.
- **An exact window needs a known starting price.** FAST, SECOND-WAVE and the fresh-crossing check require unbroken exact observation over the window and an exact price already standing at its start. Observations that only begin inside the window do not make it exact.
- **Compression never changes a decision price.** Beyond `RAW_CAP`, merged same-second entries keep their lowest, highest, first and last price and their first and last time; the newest observation is never merged; an exact window cannot start inside a merged second.

## older-6 (21 Nov 2026, 16:00 UK — owner's decision): three faster volume-breakout books

| Family | High to break | Signal volume | Baseline (median bucket) | History needed |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| HH30-5M | preceding 30 minutes | last 5 completed minutes | 24 five-minute buckets = the 2 hours before | 2 h 5 min |
| HH30-10M | preceding 30 minutes | last 10 completed minutes | 12 ten-minute buckets = the 2 hours before | 2 h 10 min |
| HH15-5M | preceding 15 minutes | last 5 completed minutes | 12 five-minute buckets = the 1 hour before | 1 h 5 min |

Rule, identical in form to HH-BREAK: price strictly above the high of the preceding window, in the band, and signal volume ≥ 2 × the median baseline bucket. Half-open windows on the last completed minute boundary; candle selection as in HH-BREAK. A zero baseline (the median bucket had no volume) is recorded and not entered. **No dollar floor (owner's decision).** Known limit, tested and on record: a coin with one small trade in at least half of its baseline buckets has a tiny non-zero norm, so a new high on one more small trade qualifies. The volume, buys and buyers in the 1 / 3 / 5 minutes before every entry are recorded, so the effect of any floor can be measured afterwards from the same buys. PumpSwap live only (price polling has no volume). One entry per family per coin per age group, as everywhere.

20 families × 4 ages × 5 stops × 2 exit policies = **800 comparisons**.

## older-7 (21 Nov 2026, 16:40 UK — owner's decision): HH-BREAK-4H, a clock book

**HH-BREAK-4H** shares every HH-BREAK buy (one entry, as CONT-STALL shares CONT's) and carries its own exit versions: the same five trailing stops and two exit policies, plus one rule — **4 hours after the fill, a position still open is sold** (0.7 s later, on the pool state in force, like every sale; reason "clock"). A stop that triggers before 4 hours works exactly as in HH-BREAK. HH-BREAK itself is unchanged. Clock books are declared in `spec.mjs CLOCK` (CONT-STALL: 60 minutes, only if the position never reached 1.15×; HH-BREAK-4H: 4 hours, unconditional). 21 families, **840 comparisons**.

Evidence behind it (84 HH-BREAK buys, minute-bar replay, 21 Nov 16:35 UK): 74 of 84 open on the 40% stop; time to 2× for the seven that got there: 40, 56, 82, 144, 176, 287, 297 minutes; a 1- or 2-hour clock would have cut the book's result to about −0.1 / +0.3 SOL against +0.7 to +1.2 as it stood, a 4-hour clock about +1.0. One day, a handful of coins: not a tuned number.

**Existing buys are carried into the new book (owner's request), once, at start-up** (`migrate.mjs`): a version that had stopped out before its 4-hour mark keeps its real result; a position younger than 4 hours is copied live and the engine runs its clock; a position past its 4-hour mark gets an **estimated** clock sale — price from the last one-minute bar close at or before the mark (no bar since the buy = unchanged), sold on the constant-product pool with the buy pool's k at that price, fee and transaction cost charged. Estimated sales carry `exit.est = true`, `exec: "estimated-minute-bar"` and are marked "est." and counted on the page. Limits: a minute close is not the state 0.7 s after the mark; the bar ratio is in USD; k is assumed unchanged. On a fresh database nothing is estimated.

## older-8 (21 Nov 2026, 21:15 UK — owner's decisions): eight any-age books

These books are evaluated **from migration on** — including the first 8 hours of a coin's life, a new age group **A0** used only by them — and in the four existing age groups. Every other book still starts at 8 hours. Migrated coins only (the feed sees the canonical PumpSwap pool and nothing before it), live observation only. One entry per family per coin per age group. Same fills, stops, exit policies and 24-hour limit as every book.

| Family | Rule |
|---|---|
| VOL300K | USD volume of the exact observations in the last 5 minutes ≥ $300,000 |
| VOL300K-UP | the same, and the price above the exact price 5 minutes ago |
| VOL500K / VOL500K-UP | the same at $500,000 |
| RUG450 | highest observed market cap ≥ $450,000, and the market cap freshly crosses down to 20% of that peak (80% below it): bought at the crossing |
| RUG450-BOUNCE | armed by that crossing; follows the low; bought at the first fresh crossing up through 1.30 × the low, within 24 hours of arming |
| RUG950 / RUG950-BOUNCE | the same with a $950,000 peak (the books nest: such a coin also qualifies for the 450 books, on one shared entry) |

- **VOL band:** $40K–$2M at the buy (the owner's limit for these four; all other books $40K–$1M). The 5-minute window must be fully observed (no feed outage, no loss of the coin's data inside it), otherwise nothing is decided.
- **RUG band:** $40K–$1M at the buy. A crossing or a bounce outside the band is not bought; a bounce spends the setup either way.
- **Peak:** the highest market cap this program has observed for the coin **without a break in observation** (see the seventh-review rules below). Peaks from before the coin was watched are unknown, so a coin that crashed earlier is not armed.
- No time limit between peak and fall (the owner asked for none); `fall_s` (peak to crossing) and `wait_s` (arming to bounce) are recorded on each entry so slow bleeds and fast collapses can be separated afterwards. VOL entries record the 5-minute volume and the 5-minute price change.

29 families: 21 × 4 age groups + 8 × 5 age groups = 124 book-ages × 5 stops × 2 exit policies = **1,240 comparisons**.

## Data-trust rules added after the seventh independent review (22 Nov 2026, 00:10 UK)

1. **RUG state is memory only, and is evidence only over unbroken observation.** The peak, the armed setup and its low live in the running program and nowhere else: nothing about them is stored with the coin. They are discarded when the program restarts, when the feed drops, when the coin's data is lost, and when the coin changes observation method. A RUG entry therefore needs the peak, the fall and (for the bounce books) the low and the rise to have been watched in one unbroken stretch. A setup abandoned this way is recorded ("abandoned: observation broke during the setup"). Observation methods that cannot support the RUG books (sampled quotes) build no RUG state at all.
2. **A price window needs a known starting price.** For windows that take a low or a high over a period (LOW4H's 4 hours, CONT's hour, the HH books' high window, the recorded measurements), "nothing was missed" is not enough: the price at the start of the window must be known as well — an observation at or before the window start, inside the same unbroken stretch. A long quiet period that was watched throughout still counts: the last price before it stands. Volume windows keep the coverage rule alone.
3. **VOL: volume at a merged second.** When a coin's history has been compressed and the 5-minute cut-off falls inside a merged second, the volume on each side of the cut-off cannot be separated. The VOL books then record "volume not known, not decided" and decide nothing at that trade; they never use a figure that is too low or too high.
4. **An entry and its one-buy flags are written in one commit.** On start-up every stored entry is checked against its flags and missing flags are restored before anything is evaluated.
5. **Estimated sales are not results.** A sale that was estimated rather than observed (the older HH-BREAK-4H sales carried in on 21 Nov) is shown on its own line and is excluded from every total, ranking, win rate and period figure. The estimate uses the last minute bar completed before the clock time, never the minute containing it and never a rejected back-fill bar. A fresh database contains none.
6. **Display:** a half-sold position's "high while held" is the best the position was actually worth (banked half + the rest), not the original position's high; a sale's market cap is always marked approximate (it uses the SOL price and supply recorded at the buy); a book's "counting since" is set when the program starts, not when the page is first opened.

## Transfer-fee coins (22 Nov 2026, 00:40 UK — owner's decision)

Until now every Token-2022 coin with a transfer fee above zero was blocked. From this version:

- **Allowed when the fee is at most 5%** (`XFER_FEE_MAX_BPS = 500`); above 5% the coin stays blocked. The fee used is the higher of the fee in force and the fee already scheduled on the mint; the mint's per-transfer cap is ignored, so the fee is never understated.
- **Deducted twice from the paper result:** off the tokens received at the buy, and off the proceeds of every sale (half sale and final sale). A 5% fee therefore costs about 9.75% of the trade on top of the ordinary costs.
- **The stop works on what the position is really worth.** A fee coin starts below its cost by about two fees, so its trailing stop has less room: on a 5% fee and a 20% stop, roughly an 11% fall from the buy price closes it.
- **Sampled method only.** The fee is applied to the routed quotes. On the live (pool-state) method the fee is not modelled, so a fee coin there is not entered.
- **Assumption, declared:** the routed quote is treated as NOT already including the transfer fee. If a router does net it, these results are too low by up to two fees — never too high. To be measured on the first fee-coin trades.
- **A separate book (owner, 22 Nov 00:48):** every buy of a fee coin is shown in one book, **FEE-COINS**, with the rule(s) that bought it, one row per buy, and is left out of the rule books' totals and out of the 1,240 report rows. FEE-COINS is a reporting group, not a rule: it adds no entry condition and no comparison row. Every such trade carries `token.xfer_bps`, an asterisk and its fee on the page; `entries.json` carries `fee`.
- **Fee coins are bought in their first 8 hours too (owner, 22 Nov 01:22).** A coin with a fee of at most 5% is evaluated from its first pool on (age group A0) by the same sampled-method rules — FAST, FAST150, LOW4H, SLOW-BUILD, CONT, CONT-STALL, SECOND-WAVE; AGE-DOUBLE has no age-group start under 8 hours and is not evaluated there. Every other sampled coin, and every book's own rows, still start at 8 hours. These buys appear only in FEE-COINS (the page's "under 8h" filter separates them). No evidence exists yet for these rules on coins under 8 hours.
- **Unchanged:** coins with a transfer hook, a permanent delegate, pausable or non-transferable tokens, confidential transfers or frozen default accounts stay blocked whatever their fee.
- Claude advised a limit of 1–2%; the owner chose 5% "for now", to be revisited with data.

## Warm restart (22 Nov 2026, 01:05 UK — owner's decision)

Until now every restart emptied the price memory, so LOW4H and HH-BREAK could not buy for about 4 hours after one, the HH30 books for about 2 hours and HH15 for about 1 hour. On 21 Nov the program was restarted about ten times and LOW4H was able to buy for roughly 25 minutes between 14:20 and midnight. From this version:

- **A planned stop saves the price memory** (`warm.mjs`): for every observed coin its exact observations, minute bars, anchor and coverage marks, and its in-memory signal state (age-group references, a SECOND-WAVE setup, the RUG peak and armed setup). Nothing is rounded or summarised. **The next start reads it back** if the file is complete and at most 60 seconds old (`WARM_MAX_GAP_S`). The file is used once.
- **The time away is still not observed.** It stays in the coverage as a gap from the stop to the moment the feed is up again. If that is at most 60 seconds the gap is marked *warm*; if longer it is an ordinary outage and nothing may span it (the start is then as cold as before for every window that reaches back past it).
- **A warm gap is tolerated by hour-scale windows only** — LOW4H / SLOW-BUILD / LOW4H-LIQ25K (4 hours), CONT / CONT-STALL (1 hour), HH-BREAK, HH-BREAK-4H and the three faster HH books (their high and volume windows), AGE-DOUBLE's reference, and the RUG books' peak-to-now stretch. This is a **declared approximation**: a low, a high or some volume inside those seconds is not known.
- **Exact windows and fresh crossings never span it** (`Series.continuous` is unchanged): the FAST and FAST150 books, SECOND-WAVE, the VOL books, and the "previous price" of every crossing. So the first trade after a restart can never be a buy; the FAST books need their own window watched again (1 to 30 minutes); a SECOND-WAVE setup alive at the stop is abandoned.
- **Every decision that relied on a window containing a warm gap is marked**: `restart_gap_s` on the decision, `warm_gap_s` and a flag on the entry, "restart N s" next to the coin on the page, `warm` in `entries.json`.
- **RUG books:** the exact peak and armed setup come back (not a stored, rounded copy — the seventh review's fault). A collapse or a bounce that happens while the program is away is not bought: the crossing must be watched. Away for more than 60 seconds: the state is dropped as before.
- **Sampled coins:** their history is read back and judged by the ordinary hole rule (a pause of more than 3 sampling intervals + 5 s restarts the history), using the interval the coin was actually sampled at before the stop; the gap itself is marked the same way.
- **A crash, a kill or a power loss writes no file: cold start, as before.** A damaged, cut-short, too old or wrong-format file is ignored: cold start. A bounded validation run never uses it. `OLDER_WARM=0` switches it off.
- **The scored seven-day run starts on a fresh database with no snapshot, and is not meant to be restarted.** If it is, the affected decisions are marked and can be left out.

## Rules added after the eighth independent review (22 Nov 2026, 02:00 UK)

**Fee coins**
1. **Unknown is not "no fee".** A sampled coin is entered only when its transfer fee is known: a classic SPL token (the program has no transfer fee), or a mint whose extensions have been read from the chain. A Token-2022 mint not read yet, or a coin whose token program is not known, is not evaluated for entry (recorded once per coin). Mints are read in this order: a coin with a position, then coins being priced (most recent first), then — every 10 minutes — coins we hold again.
2. **Eligibility is checked again at the fill.** A pending buy on a coin that has become restricted (fee over 5%, any other restriction) or whose fee is no longer known is cancelled, not filled.
3. **One classification.** The fee recorded on an entry is the highest fee actually applied to any of its quotes; FEE-COINS and the exclusion from the rule books and the 1,240 rows follow that one value.
4. **The fee is charged exactly once per transfer, under a stated assumption** (`XFER_FEE_IN_QUOTE`, currently `false`: the quote is treated as not including the fee, and the fee is deducted here). Every fill and every sale keeps the raw quoted amount, the fee, the route and the assumption. **Whether quotes include the fee is not yet measured** (`scripts/fee-probe.mjs`, read-only). Until it is, **FEE-COINS is an accounting scenario, not scored after-cost performance**, and says so on the page. A wrong assumption does not only shift the result by a constant: it moves the stops.

**Warm restart**
5. Every coverage gap is kept in the snapshot, however old. State whose provenance no longer holds at the stop (a RUG peak or setup that is not steady up to the stop, a SECOND-WAVE setup whose exact coverage broke) is not written.
6. An outage already under way at the stop is an ordinary outage from its start to the stop; only the stretch from the stop to the feed coming back can be a warm gap.
7. A coin's own price break that has aged out of the record still counts for any stretch that reaches back before its end (`Series.ownCut`).
8. The restart mark follows the observation that supplied the price standing at a window's start, not the window's nominal start.

**Reporting**
9. Estimated sales are outside the full table's totals too: counted and summed in their own column.

## older-9 (22 Nov 2026, 17:40 UK — owner's decision): buying again

Until now a book bought a coin once per age group, whether or not it had sold. The owner: "if the coin is out, it can always be bought back in … whatever stop loss we've put on the token"; waiting time: "no wait".

- **The first buy is unchanged.** One first buy per book, coin and age group, decided exactly as in older-8, carrying every row (5 stops × 2 exits, plus a clock book's own rows). A test replays random markets with buying-again on and off and requires identical first buys.
- **After it, the book may buy the coin again** whenever its rule fires and it no longer holds the coin. **Each row is judged on its own**: a row is held while its buy is pending or the row has not sold. The 20% row can be back in while the 60% row still holds the first buy.
- **A re-buy is a real new buy** — its own signal, its own fill 1 second later, its own price — and **carries only the rows that were free**. On the other rows it does not exist. Books that fire together and have the same free rows share one re-buy; a clock book (CONT-STALL, HH-BREAK-4H) can re-buy on its own rows while its base book still holds.
- **No waiting time.** A rule that describes a state rather than a crossing (the VOL books, CONT) can therefore fire again at the next trade after a stop-out; in a fall on heavy volume the VOL books buy again repeatedly. That is the owner's choice and its cost is measured, not hidden.
- **Marked and reported apart.** A re-buy carries `again` per book; the page marks it "re-buy", shows per book how much of its total comes from re-buys, and has a switch "buys: all | first only". **The 1,240 comparison rows remain first buys only**; re-buys are counted and summed beside each row (`rebuys_closed`, `rebuys_net_sol`, `rebuys_open`). `entries.json` carries `again`.
- **SECOND-WAVE:** the first setup per age group is seeded only by a first FAST buy, as before. A setup seeded again is marked, never uses up a first slot, and gives way to a first setup.
- **Safety limit (server protection, not strategy):** at most 50 re-buys of one coin by one book in 24 hours (`REBUY_MAX_PER_DAY`), recorded when it bites. Kept in memory: it starts again after a restart.
- **Unchanged:** a first buy in a new age group is still made even if the book's earlier buy on that coin is open (older-8 allowed it).
- **Switch:** `OLDER_REBUY=0` runs a database without buying again (older-8 behaviour).

## Fee inclusion measured (22 Nov 2026, 21:13 UK)

`scripts/fee-probe.mjs` was run on the server (quotes only, 0.2 SOL in, sell the quoted tokens back). Fee coins quoted: BERN 4.2% (FluxBeam), BANKROLL, MEOWL, STWC, KNOTS, NEARKAT, FEELSGOO, CYPHERCA all 3% (Raydium CP, Raydium CLMM, Whirlpool, Meteora DLMM, BisonFi, Kipseli and mixed routes). Round-trip loss 7.88%–10.18%. Control coins without a fee: 0.20%, 1.84%, 2.40%, 2.76% (median 2.40%). Extra loss over the control median: 5.47%–7.78%, against 2 × fee of 6.00% (8.40% for BERN) — within about 1.6 points in every one of the 8 cases, and nowhere near zero. Five fee coins and three controls returned no quote (rate limited: the probe shares the bot's key).

**Conclusion: the routed quotes already include the coin's transfer fee on both legs.** `XFER_FEE_IN_QUOTE = true`: the quoted amounts are used as they are and no further fee is deducted. The raw amounts, the fee and the assumption stay on every fill and sale. Fee-coin buys made while the assumption was `false` (between the 21:12 restart on cecc7a2 and the restart that loads this change) had the fee deducted twice; they are identifiable by `fill.quote.xfer_in_quote = false`.

Limits of the measurement: one moment in time, 8 coins, fees of 3% and 4.2% only, 4 controls; pool fees differ by route, which is the noise of about ±1.5 points. A route that does not net the fee would have shown an extra loss near 0%; none did.


## older-10 (24 Nov 2026) — execution trust only; no strategy number changed

Ninth independent review (Codex, on the full export of 23 Nov, code 834ca71 / 982ecde). Two execution defects were reproduced
and corrected. Entry rules, thresholds, bands, ages, stops and exits are exactly those of older-9.

### A. A coin's transfer fee must be KNOWN before it is bought — on both observation methods
- "Known" (`risk.feeKnown`) = a classic SPL token (that program has no transfer fee), or a mint whose account was read from
  the chain (`risk.classifyMintAccount`). A Token-2022 mint that has not been read, or an unknown token program, is **not
  known**, and a coin in that state is **not entered**. Until older-10 this guard applied to sampled coins only; 2,269 of the
  2,271 live buys in the shakedown run were made without it (225 on mints known to be Token-2022, 2,044 with no token program
  recorded). That does not show a fee was charged on any of them; it shows fee-free execution was never established.
- The mint-account reader (`risk.riskQueue` / `riskReadOnce`, started in `main.mjs`) now serves live coins and runs whether or
  not the sampled adapter is on. Order: coins with a position; then coins a decision is waiting on (a live coin is asked for by
  its first evaluation for entry); then, every `RISK_REFRESH_S`, coins that are held. One `getMultipleAccounts` call per round.
- Unchanged policy: a coin that charges a transfer fee is supported on the sampled method only (the fee is inside its routed
  quotes); on the live method it is not entered. A restricted coin is entered on neither.
- A pending live buy is checked again when it is about to fill: restricted, fee-bearing or no-longer-known → **cancelled**,
  with the reason on the entry (as the sampled method already did).
- A fee or restriction found on a coin that is already held on the live method is written on the position as a flag. The
  position, its exits and any unresolved exposure stay; nothing is deleted and no sale is invented.
- Declared limits: the classification is cached and refreshed only while a position is held (same as the sampled method). A
  Token-2022 fee change is announced in the mint account before it takes effect and the higher of the two fees is used, so a
  read does not understate the schedule known at that read. It says nothing about an update announced after it; the age of
  the reading is recorded on every entry and fill (section C). A transfer-hook extension with no program set is not blocking (existing rule) and
  could be armed later; it is caught by the refresh while held and by the check at the fill.

### B. Stream freshness (`FEED_MAX_LAG_S = 5`) — an operating limit, not a strategy filter
*(State machine corrected after Codex's review of commit 3537ada — findings B1, B3, B4. The three 5-second figures are
declared, frozen paper-execution assumptions. They were not fitted to results and are not claimed to be optimal.)*

- Our clock is receive time. Every decoded transaction also carries its block time, so `receive − block` measures how far
  behind the chain the stream is at that moment (normally 1.0–1.3 s). On 23 Nov the stream stayed connected and ran up to
  112 s behind; 105 buys were signalled more than 5 s late while their pool state looked 1 s old.
- **One state machine** (`engine.stream`) serves both pricing (`freshAt` → `trustedAt`) and signal coverage (`cov`, reason
  `stream`). The stream is **current**, or **interrupted** for one of three causes:
  - `connect` — start-up or a reconnect: the delay on this connection has not been measured. *Unknown is not current.* It
    ends at the **first good measurement on the connection** (the initial-connection policy, kept as written). Nothing before
    that measurement is treated as current — the time between a connect and its first measurement is not back-dated.
  - `late` — a measurement above 5 s.
  - `silence` — no transaction at all for more than 5 s while connected; it starts at the last evidence.
  - `late` and `silence` end only after a **good-evidence period** of 5 s: it starts at the first good measurement and
    starts again after a bad measurement, after more than 5 s without a measurement, or after a disconnect. A connection
    whose first measurement is late owes the full period. One good measurement after a quiet stretch ends nothing.
- **Order inside one transaction** (`engine.tx`, used by `feed.mjs`): (1) its delay is noted — this can *start* an
  interruption at once, but can only *propose* the end of one; (2) all of the transaction's committed pool states are
  installed; (3) only then does the end of the interruption take effect, and every open live position is valued once on the
  pool state **now** in force. A recovery therefore never prices an order on a state that the recovering transaction itself
  replaced, and a transaction that touches several pools is never valued halfway through.
- **At the end of an interruption** each open live position whose pool state is trusted has, in this order: (a) its due
  clock transitions run — a maximum-hold or clock-book deadline that passed during the interruption is triggered at its own
  time, whether or not the clock ran meanwhile; (b) one valuation on the state now in force, which prices every overdue
  order with the delay it really had. Both go through the eligibility gate and the ordinary trust test. *(Added after
  Codex's review of 161d545: without (a), a deadline that passed while the clock was held up left the sale pending.)*
- **A silence is noticed by the clock** (`engine.tick`), not only when the next transaction arrives: 5 s after the last
  measurement the stream is interrupted, signal coverage is down and the status says so while it is happening.
- While the stream is not current: pool states are still rebuilt, but an observation creates no price history, no signal,
  no peak and no stop, and no order is priced from a pool state. A pending buy meets the existing 5-second pricing timeout
  (cancelled, counted as a failed fill). A triggered sale stays pending and is priced by the first current state, with the
  delay it really had; the 24-hour unresolved rule is unchanged.
- For **signals** every interruption — connect, late **and silence** — is a coverage gap exactly like an outage: no exact
  window and no fresh crossing spans it. Hour-scale windows follow the declared policy for any non-warm gap (a window that
  contains the gap is not proven; back-filled minute bars may prove it under `BACKFILL_MAX_HOLE_S`). Each gap keeps its
  cause in the feed log.
- For **pool-state trust** a late spell or a silence is not in itself a gap: if events were complete and continuous, a
  pool's state is valid again once the stream is current. Reconnects, lost events, overflow, chain discontinuities and
  quarantined states keep their own, stronger requirements, unchanged.
- **Every path that can fill a pending live buy** — the event path, the clock path and the valuation after a recovery —
  passes the same eligibility gate (`engine._blocked`): restricted, fee-bearing or no-longer-known → cancelled with the
  reason. A position that is already filled is never cancelled by it.
- This is not a rule about quiet pools. An old, unchanged pool state remains a valid price while other transactions keep
  the stream's delay measured.
- **What the check can and cannot see.** A *delivered* transaction that is too old is detected whichever pool it belongs
  to, even while other transactions are current. An update that *has not arrived* cannot be detected by this check, and one
  good measurement does not prove that every pool is synchronised. Per-pool evidence remains the chain check on consecutive
  reserves and the targeted comparison of fills against the chain.
- **Gap repair** (`backfill.gapRequests`, called from `main.mjs`): the back-fill scheduler reads the *signal-coverage* gap
  list — the one hour-scale windows are tested against — so reconnects, late spells and silences are all sent for repair,
  each once, with gaps in touching minutes merged into one request. It used to read the pool-trust list, which never
  contains a late spell or a silence, and whose reconnect interval is slightly shorter than the signal gap (so a repair
  recorded for it did not cover the gap). Repair is by 1-minute bars: it can satisfy hour-scale windows only, never an
  exact window or a fresh crossing, and it does not touch pool-state trust.
- Recorded, bounded: status `stream` — as of the time of the report (`as_of`, `since_last_measurement_s`, `seconds_total`) — state, newest delay, number of measurements, mean and largest delay, a fixed
  8-bucket histogram of the delay (≤1, ≤2, ≤3, ≤5, ≤10, ≤30, ≤60, >60 s), wall-clock seconds spent current / late /
  recovering / silent / unknown, the count and the longest of each kind of interruption, the last 10 interruptions; feed
  log rows `late` / `silence` / `current`; `engine.lateEvents`. Each buy still stores its own block time and receive time.

### C. Mint-account reader — failures and age (Codex's review of 3537ada, finding B5 and limitation 3)
- A coin is marked read only when a mint account was actually parsed. A whole-batch failure (`transport`), an answer shorter
  than the request (`short_response`), an empty account (`null_account`), an account that could not be parsed
  (`unparsed`) and an account with a field of an unexpected shape (`malformed` — it fails closed: never read as "no
  extensions", and it fails that one coin only) are counted separately in status `risk_reader`, and the coin is asked for again after 60 s, doubling to at
  most one hour (for a coin we hold: never longer than the normal refresh interval). One bad account does not affect the
  others in its batch. Evidence already held for a coin is kept when a later read fails.
- A coin whose fee is not known is still **not entered**, and a crossing that was blocked is never back-dated once the
  classification arrives: a coin is asked for at its **first evaluation for entry**, normally long before any crossing, and
  a crossing seen while it was unknown is not fresh afterwards. Status counts the blocked evaluations (an upper bound on
  lost crossings — the entry rules are not run on an ineligible coin) and coins, the time from first request to classification
  (mean, largest, fixed 8-bucket histogram), the provider's round-trip time, the queue and its oldest waiting request, and
  how overdue each refresh of a held coin was.
- Each classification stores when it was read (`risk.read_at`); each entry and each live fill stores the age of the reading
  it relied on (`token.risk_age_s`, `fill.risk_age_s`). A reading takes the higher of the two scheduled Token-2022 fees —
  that covers the schedule known at that read, **not** an update announced afterwards.
- A live position on a coin that is later found fee-bearing or restricted keeps its ledger, its stops and its exposure.
  Its closed rows are counted apart (`unsupported_fee_exits`, `unsupported_fee_net_sol`; for a re-buy
  `unsupported_fee_rebuys`, `unsupported_fee_rebuys_net_sol`) and are in no primary total and no re-buy total: the
  modelled exit had no transfer fee in it. Nothing is deleted or zeroed.

Tests: `test/review9.test.mjs` (12), `test/review10.test.mjs` (13) and `test/review11.test.mjs` (9). Fixtures in `test/helpers.mjs` declare what a real feed
supplies (classic SPL tokens; a stream measured 1 s behind) and can be switched off per test; the production guards are not
bypassed. `review10` and `review11` use neither: they build the production `Engine` directly and drive it one transaction at a time, in
time order, through `engine.tx` — start-up, silence, late spells and recovery are not assumed.

====================================================================================================
FILE: older/scripts/fee-probe.mjs
====================================================================================================
// fee-probe.mjs — READ-ONLY measurement: does a Jupiter quote already include a coin's Token-2022 transfer fee?
//   node older/scripts/fee-probe.mjs [run=shakedown] [how-many=8]
// For a few coins with a known transfer fee, and a few without one as a control, it asks Jupiter for (1) the price, (2) a quote to
// buy with 0.2 SOL, (3) a quote to sell exactly the tokens that buy quote promised. Nothing is bought, sold, signed or sent: these
// are the same quote requests the paper bot already makes. The database is opened read-only. No key is printed or written.
// HOW TO READ IT: the round trip (SOL back ÷ SOL in) always loses two pool fees and some price impact. If quotes ALREADY INCLUDE
// the coin's fee, a fee coin loses about 2 × its fee MORE than the control coins do; if they do NOT, it loses about the same.
// Output: a table on screen and <OUT>/pub/fee-probe.json (picked up by the 10-minute publisher).
import { readFileSync, existsSync, writeFileSync, mkdirSync } from "node:fs";
for (const f of [process.env.OLDER_ENV || "/root/older/.env", "/root/tracker/.env"]) if (existsSync(f)) for (const l of readFileSync(f, "utf8").split("\n")) { const m = l.match(/^\s*([A-Z0-9_]+)\s*=\s*(.*)\s*$/); if (m && process.env[m[1]] === undefined) process.env[m[1]] = m[2].replace(/^["']|["']$/g, ""); }
const { default: Database } = await import("better-sqlite3"); const J = await import("../src/jupiter.mjs");
const RUN = process.argv[2] || "shakedown", N = Number(process.argv[3] || 8), OUT = process.env.OLDER_OUT || "/mnt/volume_1794164707392/older", STAKE = 0.2;
const db = new Database(`${OUT}/older-${RUN}.db`, { readonly: true, fileMustExist: true });
const fee = [], plain = [];
for (const r of db.prepare("SELECT mint, info FROM coins WHERE info LIKE ?").iterate("%solana-wide%")) { let k; try { k = JSON.parse(r.info); } catch { continue; } if (!Number.isInteger(k.decimals) || k.unpriced || k.pruned_low) continue;
  const rk = k.risk || {}, m = (rk.blocking || []).map((b) => /^transferFee (\d+) bps$/.exec(b)).find(Boolean), bps = rk.fee_bps > 0 ? rk.fee_bps : m ? Number(m[1]) : 0, other = (rk.blocking || []).some((b) => !/^transferFee /.test(b));
  if (bps > 0 && bps <= 1000 && !other) fee.push({ mint: r.mint, sym: k.symbol ?? null, dec: k.decimals, bps, origin: k.origin ?? null });
  else if (!bps && (rk.status === "clean" || rk.status === "flagged") && rk.src === "rpc mint account") plain.push({ mint: r.mint, sym: k.symbol ?? null, dec: k.decimals, bps: 0, origin: k.origin ?? null }); }
fee.sort((a, b) => b.bps - a.bps); // the largest fees first: they separate the two cases most clearly
const pct = (x) => (x == null ? "  n/a" : (x * 100).toFixed(2).padStart(6) + "%"), rows = []; let keys = null;
async function one(c, kind) {
  const p = await J.prices([c.mint, J.WSOL]).catch(() => null), px = p?.prices.get(c.mint)?.px ?? null, sol = p?.prices.get(J.WSOL)?.px ?? null; if (!(px > 0)) return null; // not priced now: skip
  const b = await J.quote(c.mint, "buy", STAKE * 1e9); if (!b.ok) return { ...c, kind, err: "buy: " + b.why };
  const s = await J.quote(c.mint, "sell", b.out); if (!s.ok) return { ...c, kind, err: "sell: " + s.why }; keys ??= b.keys;
  const tok = b.out / 10 ** c.dec, back = s.out / 1e9;
  return { ...c, kind, round_trip_loss: +(1 - back / STAKE).toFixed(5), buy_vs_price: sol > 0 ? +(1 - (tok * px) / (STAKE * sol)).toFixed(5) : null, sell_vs_price: sol > 0 ? +(1 - (back * sol) / (tok * px)).toFixed(5) : null, route_buy: b.route, route_sell: s.route, router: b.router ?? null, impact_buy: b.impact ?? null };
}
for (const [list, kind, want] of [[fee, "fee", N], [plain, "control", Math.max(4, Math.ceil(N / 2))]]) { let got = 0, tried = 0; for (const c of list) { if (got >= want || tried >= want * 4) break; tried++; const r = await one(c, kind); if (!r) continue; rows.push(r); if (!r.err) got++; } }
const ok = rows.filter((r) => !r.err), med = (a) => { const s = [...a].sort((x, y) => x - y); return s.length ? s[Math.floor(s.length / 2)] : null; }, base = med(ok.filter((r) => r.kind === "control").map((r) => r.round_trip_loss));
console.log(`fee-probe: ${ok.filter((r) => r.kind === "fee").length} fee coins, ${ok.filter((r) => r.kind === "control").length} control coins (0.2 SOL in, quotes only)`);
console.log("kind     coin      fee     round trip   minus control   2 x fee   reading                route (buy)");
for (const r of rows) { if (r.err) { console.log(`${r.kind.padEnd(8)} ${String(r.sym || r.mint.slice(0, 6)).slice(0, 8).padEnd(8)} ${pct(r.bps / 1e4)}  no quote (${r.err.slice(0, 50)})`); continue; }
  const extra = base != null ? r.round_trip_loss - base : null, two = (2 * r.bps) / 1e4; r.extra_vs_control = extra != null ? +extra.toFixed(5) : null;
  r.reading = r.kind !== "fee" || extra == null ? "" : extra >= 0.75 * two ? "fee looks INCLUDED" : extra <= 0.25 * two ? "fee looks NOT included" : extra >= 0.35 * two && extra <= 0.65 * two ? "fee on ONE leg only?" : "unclear";
  console.log(`${r.kind.padEnd(8)} ${String(r.sym || r.mint.slice(0, 6)).slice(0, 8).padEnd(8)} ${pct(r.bps / 1e4)}  ${pct(r.round_trip_loss)}      ${pct(extra)}        ${pct(two)}   ${r.reading.padEnd(22)} ${(r.route_buy || []).join(" > ").slice(0, 40)}`); }
console.log(`control round trip (median): ${pct(base)}   quote response fields: ${(keys || []).join(", ")}`);
mkdirSync(`${OUT}/pub`, { recursive: true }); writeFileSync(`${OUT}/pub/fee-probe.json`, JSON.stringify({ at: new Date().toISOString(), run: RUN, stake_sol: STAKE, control_round_trip_median: base, quote_fields: keys, rows }));
console.log(`written: ${OUT}/pub/fee-probe.json`); process.exit(0);

FILE: older/scripts/go11.sh

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# older-11 — start the VALIDATION run (paper only, NOT scored) on a new database, with history repair paused so that the
# provider's allowance can be measured with buy-checks alone. Nothing is deleted: the previous run's database stays as it is.
#   bash older/scripts/go11.sh [name=valid11] [previous run=shakedown]
# Afterwards:  rm <out>/repair.off   switches history repair on (no restart);   touch <out>/repair.off   pauses it again.
# The scored run is started later, from the validation run:  bash older/scripts/start.sh score11 7 older-11 valid11
set -euo pipefail
HERE="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")/.." && pwd)"; OUT="${OLDER_OUT:-/mnt/volume_1794164707392/older}"; NAME="${1:-valid11}"; PREV="${2:-shakedown}"
mkdir -p "$OUT"; touch "$OUT/repair.off"
bash "$HERE/scripts/start.sh" "$NAME" "" older-11 "$PREV"
echo "history repair is PAUSED (file $OUT/repair.off). Buy-checks are the only GMGN calls now."
